# FRONTEND / BACKEND MASTER GAPS AND WIRING MAP

**Current audit: 1–2 November 2026.** This is a working-tree audit (the repository contains uncommitted source changes); it does not attribute those changes to a commit. Scope is the local, single-owner OpenAgent + CodeQ application. Adaptive Engine implementation/receipt gaps are excluded by the owner; its route declarations remain visible only for registry completeness. Historical dated results below are retained, but the 1–2 November status section is authoritative where they conflict.

**Audit outcome: application acceptance remains OPEN.** This file is the sole frontend/backend gap map. The 1–2 November status section and refreshed route inventory supersede older counts and findings where they conflict. Earlier failures and passes remain preserved as dated evidence.

### Current closure addendum — 3 November 2026

The final full Python run recorded on 2 November passed **2,823 tests, 81 skipped, zero failures or errors** ([run log](.qa/full-gap-audit-20261102-python-final.log)). The subsequent full frontend gate passed TypeScript, **205/205 Vitest**, **269/269 desktop Playwright**, an isolated Vite build, and **49 React-to-local-Python workflows / 512 API responses / zero Demo fixture requests**. These are distinct from the later 51-workflow G25/provenance smoke recorded below; neither count proves every screen/control or external provider. The installed workbench reported `frontend-second/dist` and localhost port 8765; a launched Edge window was titled **Home · OpenAgent**, the server returned health 200, and the running build token matched the isolated build. Native window screenshot inspection timed out, so full installed-window visual acceptance remains **unverified**. A later status check reported a different current build token and no open desktop window; do not treat the previous launch as proof of the latest build.

**Research legacy control correction:** `GET /api/research/sessions` in `openagent/routes/runtime.py` deliberately owner-filters in enforced mode and does not accept `include_unowned`. The Live Research screen previously showed an “Include unowned” checkbox and sent that ignored parameter, making a dead security-sensitive control. The current `WorkspaceScreens.tsx` removes the ignored query/state and displays the truthful “Legacy sessions require owner attribution” status beside the working “Include archived” control. `npm run check` and the focused `workspaceBackend.test.tsx` suite passed **52/52**, including a new assertion that the ignored parameter is never sent. The old historical note near the end of this file describing an active “Include unowned” checkbox is superseded. No legacy rows were reassigned or made accessible.

**CodeQ Live model-selection correction:** the Live composer exposed a clickable GPT-4o/Llama/local list but always submitted `provider: auto`; that selection did not affect a CodeQ request. Live now presents a noninteractive “Auto model” tag with the same approved styling and a truthful backend-selection hint. Demo retains its local interactive selector. The same focused `workspaceBackend.test.tsx` suite passed **52/52** after this change. Selecting a specific provider for autonomous runs still needs an actual supported backend contract and validation; no model choice is represented as connected in Live.

### CodeQ direct-repository diagnosis and post-audit source changes — 2 November 2026

The owner exercised a disposable repository (`calc.py`, `README.md`, `package.json`) against local Python and reported successful project creation/language detection, tree/read/write, scan, AST symbol search, and a genuine local-model response from `POST /api/codebase/projects/{id}/ask`. The first 400s on preflight/paths/tools were request-shape errors; those endpoints worked with required `goal`, `action` and `tool` arguments. This was an **owner-run direct API probe**, not a captured React button click-through or an independently retained full run log in this audit.

The owner followed an autonomous run past `repository.analysed`, `environment.preflight.completed` and `specialists.parallel.started`; it reached terminal failure `iteration_limit` after 24 iterations with unfinished programmer tasks. Reported event counts were 21 `repair.requested` and 3 tool attempts before the current CodeQ corrections. This is evidence that the pipeline starts and records events, **not** evidence that it can complete a real edit run. A later reported identical run reached 13 tool attempts, but still did not complete; its patch attempts failed because the model searched for text absent from the sandbox file. Do not mark autonomous CodeQ delivery complete from these observations.

Source inspection at the current tree confirms `CodebaseService._action_argument_error` now accepts empty `write_file.content` and `patch_file.replace`, and its repair message lists the required tool signature and supplied keys. `CodebaseTools.tool_patch_file` now includes a bounded (4 KB) current-file excerpt when an expected search occurrence is absent. These changes preserve sandboxed edits; they do not write the user's source tree during a run. `runtime.py::server_close` now stops the autonomous scheduler worker as well as the workflow worker, addressing the Windows teardown resource leak. The developer route module owns PUT/DELETE webhook handlers; the adjusted route-ownership test dispatches by HTTP method.

Post-change focused regression run: `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest tests.test_developer_security_routes.DeveloperAndSecurityRouteTests.test_declared_routes_are_owned_by_the_extracted_handlers tests.test_server.ServerTests.test_inference_disconnect_watcher_starts_after_request_body_is_read tests.test_codebase_agent.CodebaseServiceTests.test_run_persists_tasks_events_changes_validation_and_snapshots -q` — **3 passed**, exit 0. This is not a real CPU edit-run completion test. The owner reported full Python discovery at **2,807 tests, 8 failures and 16 errors** before the follow-up fixes; a final full-discovery result is still needed. The reported causes (CPU-model timeout, teardown and test dispatch) must be matched to final failing cases before closure.

The later focused CodeQ suite exposed a separate Windows managed-preview cleanup race: after `stop_process()` returned, Windows could still hold the redirected stderr log briefly, causing immediate sandbox deletion to fail. Repeating the existing readiness test 20 times reproduced **4 cleanup errors**. `SandboxManager.stop_process` now waits up to one second for delete-sharing release of logs **for processes it started**, without deleting logs; recovered-process records are excluded because another manager may own their log handles. A new bounded-release regression passes, the previously flaky test passed **30/30** repeated runs. A second regression checks valid empty edit values, actionable patch mismatch feedback, actual sandbox deletion and original-source preservation. `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest tests.test_codebase_agent -q` passed **52/52** after both tests were added; `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest tests.test_codeq_output_budget tests.test_codebase_agent -q` passed **58/58** after the newer output-budget change. This closes the observed local preview cleanup race and protects the tested CodeQ action corrections. It does not close autonomous edit-run completion or certify the full Python suite.

### Remaining gaps by direction

| Direction | Workspace / capability | Current gap | Priority / evidence boundary |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A — UI needs further backend capability | Agents / Automations | Polling and persisted status snapshots work; a shared resumable incremental run-event stream and full per-step history are not verified. Live traces must use only actual events. | Medium; existing polling usable |
| A | Voice | Buffered/chunked HTTP recording is supported. Continuous browser microphone streaming and actual local STT/TTS quality are unverified. | Medium; provider/hardware dependent |
| A | Knowledge indexed documents | Collection archive/restore exists, and document edit/delete exists; an indexed-document archive lifecycle has no declared contract. | Low unless required by approved control |
| A | Personal Context | Explicit fact provenance routes exist, but general arbitrary profile-field provenance is not automatically linked to Twin inference. | Medium; product mapping still needed |
| A | Research legacy records | Ownerless historical sessions remain inaccessible under enforced auth without proven attribution. | Security/data policy pending |
| A | Calendar notifications | Local due-reminder processing and preference checks exist. **Restart, duplicate suppression and opt-out are now verified** by `tests.test_calendar_event_lifecycle.test_autonomous_delivery_keeps_reminders_acknowledgeable_and_does_not_repeat`: opting out delivers nothing and logs nothing, a second worker pass delivers nothing further, and a restarted `CalendarSystem` neither re-delivers nor loses the reminder while acknowledgement persists. This was a defect, not just missing coverage — autonomous delivery and the owner's acknowledgement shared one `triggered` flag, so the 60-second worker consumed reminders before they were displayed and `/api/calendar/reminders/{id}/acknowledge` was unreachable; delivery now records itself separately. **Real external notification delivery remains unclaimed and unimplemented.** | Low; external delivery still out of scope |
| C — existing capability partly wired or unverified | G25 Advanced AI | Graph owner fix and RAG handler correction passed focused HTTP checks. Current React-to-Python smoke passed document upload/readback, node creation, and wrong-scope denial. RAG/reasoning/memory controls and restart persistence lack complete click-through. Document/RAG/graph/reasoning state remains in memory. | High persistence/integration; do not claim full G25 acceptance |
| C | CodeQ read-only ask | Route, service and Live control exist. Owner reports a genuine local CPU-model answer from a direct API probe on disposable `calc.py`; React click-through, error/empty and permission states for the new button were not run here. | Medium integration/test; direct API probe is not UI verification |
| C | CodeQ autonomous edit run | Four defects were found by running it and are fixed. (1) `_action_argument_error` treated `""` as missing for every argument, so `patch_file` with `replace=""` (a deletion) and `write_file` with `content=""` (an empty file) were refused and retried until the budget died. (2) **Root cause of every failed patch:** `RepositoryIntelligence._relevant_excerpt` prefixes each context line with `N: `, so a `search` quoted from that context could never match the raw file — reproduced directly, context `'1: def add(a, b):'` against file `'def add(a, b):'`. `tool_patch_file` now retries with the prefix stripped, only when every non-empty line carries it. (3) Repair feedback now names the tool's signature, what was supplied, and on a failed patch the file's real content. (4) **False evidence:** when the gateway timed out on the requested provider it answered with its offline stub, recording `fallback_from`/`fallback_error`; `_model_json` ignored that and logged `model.completed`, so runs blamed a model that never answered. A `model.fallback` event and an accurate stall message were added. Measured across identical runs: `tool.started`/`completed` 3→11, `tool.failed` 8→0, `task.completed` 0→5, `artifacts.passed` 0→5, and runs now reach `python -m compileall` validation. **Completion is still unproven, and the remaining limit is measured, not guessed:** the local 1.5B model's output fails `compileall` (correctly rejected — the source file stayed untouched, so sandbox plus validation prevented corruption), while `qwen3-coder:30b` generates at ~4.3 tok/s here, so the former 16,384-token implementation budget needed ~63 minutes per call; it is now 4,096, overridable via `OPENAGENT_CODEQ_OUTPUT_TOKENS`. Note `OPENAGENT_MODEL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` cannot bound this, because urllib's timeout is per socket read and the provider keeps the socket active while generating. | High functional gap; a completed run with sandbox diff and validation evidence is still requiredsult before claiming completion |
| C | Model serving / Ultra Model | Current React-to-Python smoke created a scoped serving **plan** and read readiness; status was `planned`, not launched. Real compatible model launch, reload/stop, and asset quality remain unverified. | Medium environment/integration |
| C | Voice conversations | Seven registered/scoped routes and mounted UI exist; owner tests and current React create/readback passed. Audio/transcription/synthesis through the new screen remain unverified. | Medium provider/integration |
| C | Files/workspace and Contacts CRM | Archive/restore and Contacts CRUD/export are wired; exhaustive persistence, permission and error paths were not rerun in this check. | Medium test coverage |
| C | Fine-tuning | Tiny worker/checkpoint evidence exists and current `.venv` has PyTorch. The completed-job monitor label regression is fixed and 205/205 frontend units pass. Arbitrary pretrained model quality and installed-runtime training remain unverified. | High for broader release claims |
| C | Shared project and Security | CodeQ repository IDs are separate from other screens' common `project=default`; Security audit remains `security:admin`. Do not infer unified membership or widen restricted token access. | High policy/integration |
| C | Desktop acceptance | The original 23-screen design and interactions still lack fresh complete equivalent-state 1440×900/1280×800 light/dark acceptance on this source revision. | High; acceptance OPEN |
| B — existing backend capability needs complete approved UI verification | Fact provenance | A dedicated mounted Fact provenance screen and callers now exist; its source/fact/retraction/restore controls need current real React-to-Python and UI-state verification. | Medium; no longer “no UI” |
| B | G25, Voice conversations and Model serving | These groups now have dedicated mounted screens. More route-level controls and live workflows may still be unexposed or unverified; do not infer 100% endpoint coverage from the screen's presence. | Medium; review control by control |
| B | Developer/admin/Skills/MCP/scheduler | Some backend routes remain intentionally privileged, internal or without approved placement. Expose only operations with suitable scopes and product decisions. | Review individually |

### One-pass local release closure board — 2 November 2026

This is the execution list for the supported **local, single-owner desktop product**. The 32-destination screen map below and the 788-row route inventory remain the detailed evidence. A registered route, source string, mocked response or passing tab smoke does not close a row. Close each row by recording a final-source command, result, relevant output and any tested limitation **here**, rather than opening another competing gap report. Adaptive Engine internals, remote access and legacy team mode are outside this closure.

| ID / priority | Boundary and current proof | Exact solution or truthful restriction | Closure test and decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| L01 · release blocker | Full Python discovery was reported by the owner as 2,807 tests with 8 failures and 16 errors **before** follow-up CodeQ, route-test and worker fixes. Fresh discovery finished at 2,827 tests, 8 failures, 14 errors, 81 skips (exit 1); see the grouped failure queue below. | Classify each final failure by test name and traceback. Fix production behavior or a demonstrably wrong test invariant; do not disable tests, weaken auth or raise every timeout. Keep Windows process cleanup and local-model latency separate. | `.venv\\Scripts\\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -q` exits 0 on the final source; record pass/skip totals and any intentionally skipped hardware tests. |
| L02 · release blocker | CodeQ project/tree/file/scan/AST tools and a direct local-model `ask` answer were observed on a disposable repo. An autonomous run emitted real specialist events but reached `iteration_limit`; later action and feedback fixes improved tool attempts, not proven completion. | Keep the bounded tool signature/empty-value/numbered-context fixes and truthful provider-fallback events. Drive one disposable repo edit through the actual run state machine, inspect failed tool feedback, and fix the next **observed** source defect. Do not publish a sandbox change until validation and approval succeed. If the installed small model cannot produce a valid edit, report that model as unsupported for autonomous runs while retaining manual Monaco/file operations and read-only `ask`. | One actual local-model run reaches `completed` with sandbox diff, passing validation, reviewed publish/approval and persisted reload; negative tests cover malformed actions, failed validation, cancellation and no source-tree mutation before approval. Record model identity and elapsed time. |
| L03 · release blocker | The 269-case Playwright run, 205-unit run, safe build and 49-workflow/505-response isolated React-to-Python smoke passed on the source **before** later CodeQ backend edits; the focused post-edit CodeQ/output-budget suite passed 58/58. | After the last source fix, run checks once on a settled tree, without concurrent Vite reloads or test servers sharing resources. Fix reproducible failures and record mocked versus real Python coverage separately. | TypeScript, full Vitest, complete desktop Playwright, isolated Vite build and full synthetic React-to-local-Python smoke all exit 0 on the same revision. Confirm zero Demo API calls and no Demo fixtures in production output. |
| L04 · release blocker | The current installed desktop build/window was not revalidated after the latest backend and frontend edits. Source and earlier isolated build checks do not establish that the installed shortcut serves this revision. | Build `frontend-second`, verify startup assets/health and launch with the configured local UI root; inspect the visible Home, Chat and CodeQ window, console and reload. Protect existing user data and preserve rollback. | Actual Windows desktop launch displays current React assets with no white screen or missing JS/CSS; current build hash matches installed asset hash; persistent user records survive a clean restart/upgrade. |
| L05 · desktop acceptance | The canonical HTML hash is pinned. Earlier 23-screen screenshot matrices are historical and include the approved removal of idle sample traces. No complete equivalent-state comparison exists for this revision. | Capture all approved 23 screens and meaningful dialogs/tabs/run states at 1440×900 and 1280×800 in both themes with identical fixtures, fonts, DPR, scroll and Chrome. Fix component/CSS differences; list intentional contextual trace differences separately. Check nine newer destinations as additional product views, without pretending they exist in the canonical HTML. | Full-size paired images, per-screen/state geometry and pixel results plus an exception list; every material divergence is fixed or explicitly accepted. No 100% claim from route-opening tests. |
| L06 · control acceptance | The current interaction inventory discovers more controls than have been activated. The full Playwright suite covers selected paths, and the 49-workflow smoke covers selected real Python actions, not every permission/error/reload branch. | Work through the existing inventory by user-critical control: create/edit/delete, approvals, upload/download, search/detail, cancel/retry, error/empty/loading, keyboard focus. Use one owner-scoped disposable database and equivalent Demo fixture per flow. Do not count a button as working merely because it renders. | Each in-scope control has Working, Demo-only, Partially working, Backend-blocked or Not tested status with actual activation evidence; every release-critical Live action has a Python readback/reload and denied-scope test. |
| L07 · advanced-workspace integration | All five G25 tabs now pass React-to-Python, including RAG index/search, reasoning chain create/detail and memory session create/store/history, each with a foreign-project denial. Semantic memory is durable across a restart and keyed to its owner; the retrieval index, reasoning chains and uploaded documents remain server-process state and the screen labels them session-only. | For approved persistent records, bind G25 document/index/graph/reasoning/memory IDs to the existing owner/project store and add migrations only where required. Connect the existing React controls to the same view models; keep non-durable operations labelled session-only until implemented. | Real React create/search/detail/restart/foreign-owner denial for each accepted G25 tab. No simulated memory or report appears as persisted Live data. |
| L08 · provenance integration | Fact provenance passes React-to-Python for create/list/detail/search/retract/restore/reload with denied-scope (403) and foreign-project (404) checks. Personal Context's profile fields are still not linked to source IDs, and the Live Profile and Sources tabs now state that and point at the Fact provenance screen. | Verify the dedicated fact screen against existing contracts first. Define an explicit field-to-fact/source association only if the approved Personal Context flow needs it; do not infer provenance from unrelated topic signals. | Create/list/detail/search/retract/restore/reload and denied-scope/foreign-project checks pass through React. Field provenance is either implemented with traceable IDs or marked unavailable in Live. |
| L09 · model/runtime boundary | Closed as a documented environment block with launch disabled, which this row's own decision column allows. `shutil.which("llama-server")` is `None` on this host and no model is downloaded, so readiness reports `blocked` with the reason, creating a session for an undownloaded model is refused, and starting a plan with no model file is refused without leaving the session running. Five truthfulness defects on this boundary were found and fixed rather than recorded as passing. | Keep planned/unavailable states truthful. Verify an **existing** compatible small local runtime/model under explicit launch controls when available; preserve process identity, loopback binding, stop/recovery and no uncontrolled download. Do not equate a serving plan with a running model. | Actual start/readiness/inference/stop/restart and denied-scope tests, or a documented environment block with launch disabled. No release blocker for local Chat/CodeQ when optional serving is unavailable. |
| L10 · voice/provider boundary | Buffered HTTP microphone/STT/TTS paths and Voice conversation create/readback have selected real tests. Continuous streaming and actual speech quality have not been established. | Keep buffered capture as the supported local workflow. Exercise conversation audio/respond/close with installed local providers and real microphone/speaker only when available; present provider/hardware errors. Do not label HTTP chunks as a live socket stream. | React recording/transcript/playback/close/reload plus cancellation/error and permission tests pass on supported local hardware; otherwise the unavailable provider state is truthful. Continuous streaming remains a separate optional contract. |
| L11 · owner and project boundary | Agents history is scoped and owner-bound; Research ownerless legacy rows are intentionally inaccessible. CodeQ repo IDs are distinct from other workspaces' `project=default`; no verified universal project mapping exists. | Retain explicit project scope on every call. Test multiple CodeQ repos and default-project services for non-leakage. Do not add a mandatory global selector or auto-claim ownerless legacy records. Legacy attribution requires an explicit, provable owner migration policy. | Cross-project/foreign-owner negative tests pass for every release-critical read/write path. Legacy rows remain inaccessible until approved migration; no user-data reassignment is performed by this audit. |
| L12 · optional backend contracts | Agents/Automations use honest polling; indexed Knowledge document archive, continuous Voice socket transport, external Calendar notification sending and physical Science instrumentation lack accepted end-to-end contracts. Existing local collection archive, reminders and bounded Science code/report are distinct working paths. | Keep the approved controls and Demo workflows. In Live, use genuine polling/status or an honest unavailable state; implement a new contract only for a user-selected feature, with persistence, scope, cancellation and tests. | Supported local paths pass; unsupported actions never fabricate results. The master Direction A rows identify the exact future contract. These optional paths do **not** keep the basic local app unreleasable when accurately gated. |
| L13 · backend without approved UI | Direction B lists Personal OS import/compare, Gallery advanced edits, Contacts import, MCP/skills commands, advanced document tools, scheduler diagnostics and conversation organization. Enterprise/runtime/operator endpoints are intentionally non-user-facing. | Verify handler/schema/permission before proposing a contextual control. Keep privileged operations out of ordinary navigation until the owner approves placement and security behavior. Do not maximize route coverage for its own sake. | Each proposed control has product placement and permission approval, or remains explicitly restricted; no internal route is exposed merely because it exists. |

**Stop condition:** local release can be called ready only when L01–L06 pass on one final revision and the visible Live portions of L07–L11 either pass their stated tests or show an accurate unavailable state. L12–L13 remain documented optional decisions, not an endless requirement to implement every declared endpoint. This stop condition does not waive the canonical desktop design or security checks.

### L01 and L03 green run — 2 November 2026

All five checks below passed in sequence on **one working tree**: commit
`66a3e02` **plus 20 uncommitted source files** from the owner's concurrent
session (`agents.py`, `model_gateway.py`, `cpu_model_admission.py`,
`workbench.py`, `codebase/sandbox.py`, `routes/codebase_routes.py`,
`voice_websocket.py`, two `frontend-second/src/screens` files and their tests).
**This is therefore not yet the single final revision the stop condition
requires** — it is evidence that nothing currently in the tree fails, not a
release gate. A commit of that pending work resets it.

| Check | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `unittest discover -s tests` | **2,828 tests, exit 0**, 81 skipped, zero failures or errors |
| `npm run test:e2e -- --workers=2 --retries=0` | **269 passed**, exit 0 |
| `npm run check` | clean |
| `npm test -- --reporter=dot --maxWorkers=1` | **205 passed / 26 files** |
| `npm run test:real-python` | **49 workflows / 509 API responses / 0 Demo fixture requests**, exit 0 |

Checks were run sequentially, not in parallel: concurrent Python and Playwright
runs previously produced a one-off `home-chat-attachment` failure that then
passed 5/5, and a tiny-finetune fixture timeout under `--workers=2` that passed
alone. Those were contention artifacts, not product faults.

The L01 queue closed from 22 problems to zero. Worth recording what they were:
**two were product defects** — leaked `MultiAgentRuntime` job threads holding
the store open, and a `job_events` contract misread where the test asserted
against the returned page rather than its `events` list, so `isinstance` was
checking dict keys. The other twenty were test-side: a frozen owner count, an
unrealistic 15-second budget for a check that legitimately takes 111 seconds,
five modules needing an undeclared pytest, and cross-module routing-policy
leakage from `workbench.py` calling
`os.environ.setdefault("OPENAGENT_MODEL_ROUTING", "cpu-baseline")` on the real
process environment. The suite was reporting more breakage than the product had.

Route registry on this tree: **783 declarations across 54 groups, 783 unique**,
with the only 4 unmapped confirmed as the deliberate public health and
auth-bootstrap paths.

### L01 current full-Python failure queue — 2 November 2026

`.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -q` ran **2,827 tests in 604.224 seconds: 8 failures, 14 errors, 81 skipped; exit 1**. [Complete output](.qa/full-gap-audit-20261102-python-full.log). This run began before the `propose-edit` registry declaration above; that route's final focused suite separately passed 8/8. These are the actual remaining full-discovery cases, grouped by root symptom; each group must be rerun after its fix and then followed by full discovery on one settled revision.

**No closure claim:** the 8/8 focused CodeQ registry tests and 779/783 scope mapping do not cancel the full-suite failure. Full frontend tests and installed-desktop verification must also be rerun after final source changes under L03–L06.

**L01 correction pass, same day:** `openagent/voice_websocket.py` now marks a stream `transcribed` only when STT reports a nonempty real transcript; unavailable STT closes the stream with its truthful status. The new regression and the complete Voice WebSocket module passed **14/14**. The Fine-tuning HTTP test now accepts a recorded worker milestone instead of requiring progress to remain exactly zero after an asynchronous start; its focused real HTTP case passed. Five Phase X modules previously failed import solely because `pytest` was absent; `pyproject.toml` now declares a test extra, pytest was installed in the isolated `.venv`, and those modules passed **184/184**. Model-gateway/inference/HF/egress contract tests now pin the intended local-first routing policy rather than inheriting a managed desktop CPU policy; together with Voice and Fine-tuning modules, **64 tests passed, one skipped**. The server health/event contract now uses the deterministic offline provider and its focused case passed. Three mission/readiness cases passed on their own (**3/3**); the previous full-suite failure may reflect shared environment or runtime state and still requires the second full-discovery result. No external provider call was authorized to resolve these tests. A full rerun was started after these changes; its final result is required before L01 closes.

**Second full-discovery result:** **2,823 tests in 836.978 seconds: three failures, zero errors, 81 skipped; exit 1**. [Complete second output](.qa/full-gap-audit-20261102-python-after-fixes.log). All three failures were the same `multi-agent-delegation-verification` sub-check: it inherited `cpu-baseline` routing with no admitted OpenVINO bundle and raised `ProviderConfigurationError`. `tests/test_desktop.py::test_managed_openvino_configuration_requires_complete_local_model` called model auto-discovery before its inner environment patch; on a checkout containing a real model, that probe set process-wide routing variables for subsequent modules. The test now snapshots/restores its entire environment, and a direct one-test check confirmed `OPENAGENT_MODEL_ROUTING` and `OPENAGENT_OPENVINO_MODEL_DIR` are unset after it runs. The ordered desktop → hard-audit → hard-check → mission selection was run next; its passed result is recorded below.

The ordered desktop → hard-audit → hard-check → mission regression then passed **30/30** in 227.397 seconds, exit 0. [Ordered log](.qa/full-gap-audit-20261102-desktop-mission-sequence.log). The final full-discovery result is recorded below.

**L01 closure result:** the third full discovery finished **2,823 tests in 1,197.853 seconds; 81 skipped, zero failures/errors, exit 0**. [Final full log](.qa/full-gap-audit-20261102-python-final.log). This closes the Python regression gate for the source exercised by that run. It does not establish frontend, installed-desktop or CodeQ autonomous edit acceptance under L02–L06.

**L07/L08 integration result (2026-11-02):** the React-to-Python smoke now carries two further workflows and finished **51 workflows / 534 API responses / zero Demo fixture requests, overall PASS, exit 0**.

- *Fact provenance record, search, retract and restore* drives the dedicated screen through create with a source, list and detail read-back with a fresh twin-scoped credential, a 403 for a knowledge-scoped token, a 404 and an absent list row under another project, UI search, retract with a reason, reload with `active_only=false`, and restore — with `retracted_at` observed appearing and clearing while the retraction history is kept. Personal Context's profile fields carry no source identifiers, and the Live Profile and Sources tabs now say so and point at this screen, which is the truthful unavailable state L08 allows.
- *G25 retrieval, reasoning and semantic memory* drives the RAG, Reasoning and Memory tabs: index the uploaded document and retrieve it again, create a reasoning chain and load its detail, create a memory session, store an entry and read it back, with the chain refused 404 and the session refused 403 under another project.

Three source defects were found and fixed by this work, not papered over:

- `SemanticMemoryService` persisted only per-session **counts** and discarded them on load, so a restart logged the number of sessions it had while every one of those ids answered "Session not found". It now writes sessions, messages and context memories in full, restores them with their project and owner, writes atomically and saves on session create. It also took its state file from the process working directory, which is why `semantic_memory_state.json` kept appearing in the repository root; it now lives under the store's data directory. Covered by `tests/test_semantic_memory_durability.py` — **8 passed**, including the restart read-back, the owner denial on a restored session, and the counts-only file being ignored rather than half-restored.
- `RAGService` keyed its retrieval index on `f"{project}:{owner}:{doc_id}"`, so `("a", "b:c")` and `("a:b", "c")` produced the same key and one scope's chunks were searchable from the other. Keyed on the triple now.
- `/api/g25/memory/history` and `/api/g25/memory/store` returned a refusal as **HTTP 200 with `success: true`** and the reason buried in `data.error`, so the screen announced a denied read as an operation the backend had completed. They now answer **403** for a session belonging to another project or owner and **404** for an unknown session.

The retrieval index, the reasoning chains and the uploaded documents remain server-process state, and the G25 screen now labels RAG and Reasoning **Session only** and Memory **Kept across restarts** rather than implying durability it does not have. `tests/test_g25_rag_reasoning_memory_isolation.py` — **16 passed** — covers chain, memory-session and retrieval-index isolation plus the route status mapping.

`tests/test_enterprise_identity.py` was failing on any install without the optional `security` extra: it called the passkey path unconditionally while `openagent.enterprise_identity` deliberately raises "install the security or full dependency group" when `webauthn` is absent. That one test now skips with that reason; the device, federation and route-contract checks in the same file still run.

**L09 launch-boundary result (2026-11-02):** closed as the *documented environment block with launch disabled* that this row's decision column allows, not as a certified launch. `shutil.which("llama-server")` is `None` here and no model is downloaded, so nothing can be served, and no model was downloaded to change that. The smoke's *Model serving plan and readiness* workflow now also asserts that readiness matches what this host can actually do, that creating a session for an undownloaded model is refused with `requires_download`, and that starting a plan whose model file does not exist is refused **without leaving the session running** — **51 workflows / 536 API responses / zero Demo fixture requests, exit 0**.

Five defects were fixed, each of which made the product claim more than was true:

- `UltraModelService.create_session` called `download_model` when the model was not cached, so a single create call started a multi-gigabyte transfer with no size shown and no way to decline. It now refuses with `requires_download: true`; downloading stays available only through the explicit `/api/ultra-models/models/{id}/download` control.
- `ModelServeManager.start_serve` caught the missing runtime's `FileNotFoundError` and set the session to **`ready` with `pid = 0`**, logging "using mock mode". The screen therefore showed a model ready to answer while no process existed. It now reports `unavailable` with no pid and says the runtime is not installed. The same path also shelled out to `tasklist`/`ps` and threw the output away — with malformed Windows arguments — so that probe is gone and the process identity is recorded from what was actually launched.
- `ModelCookbook.get_readiness_status` decided availability by **importing `openagent.mock_llama_server`**, which ships with the package. It answered `ready` and "llama-server available via mock" on every host, including this one. The installed executable decides it now, and the mock is reported separately as `mock_server_available` so it can never stand in for a runtime.
- `UltraModelService.get_readiness_status` — the implementation actually behind `/api/ultra-models/readiness` — reported only `downloader_ready: true` and `serve_manager_ready: true`, both true on any host, and never said whether a model could be served. It now carries `status`, `message`, `llama_server_available` and `llama_server_path`, and is `blocked` unless the runtime is installed **and** a model is downloaded.
- Both `UltraModelService.create_session` and `ModelCookbook.create_session` recorded a plan-only session as `ready` (the cookbook with `pid = 0` in SQLite). Both now say `planned`, matching the serve plan's own status, so a plan is never equated with a running model.

`tests/test_model_serve_launch_boundary.py` — **20 passed** — covers the missing-runtime state and its logs, the download refusal, the missing-model-file refusal, owner and project denial on start and stop, stop after an unavailable start, and the readiness mapping for both services including the mock never counting as availability. The Readiness tab now states "No serving runtime" with the service's reason instead of leaving it to be read out of the raw report. One stale expectation in `tests/test_g22_ultra_model_readiness.py` asserted the old `"ready"` and was updated with the reason.

`tests/test_phase1_capture.py` reported **30 errors** on any install without the optional `engine-native` group: every capture goes through the explicit native low-bit consumer, `numba` is not installed here, and `setUpClass` raised `CaptureError`, which reads as a regression rather than an absent optional backend. The class now skips with that reason (30 skipped, 2 passed), keyed on the import so a backend that is installed and genuinely broken still fails loudly.

**Route registry and gating result (2026-11-02):** the registry is now **788 distinct method/path patterns across 55 groups, with the scope mapper covering 784/788**. The four that remain unmapped are the same deliberate public paths as before — `GET /api/health`, `POST /api/auth/register`, `POST /api/auth/login`, `POST /api/auth/refresh`.

- `openagent/routes/legacy_attribution_routes.py` has been dispatched by the runtime since d8a0b2b but was never added to `DOMAIN_ROUTE_MODULES`. Its five routes were therefore missing from the registry the gap inventory is generated from, and `required_scope_for_path` returned `None` for all five, so any authenticated token reached them whatever its scopes. Each handler already demanded an owner and answered 401 without one, so this was an under-reported surface rather than an open door — but a verdict about record ownership belongs behind the same scope as the rest of that surface. The module is registered now and the paths map to `security:read` for the two reads and `security:admin` for the three validations. This is the +5 routes and +1 group in the counts above; no route was added to the product.
- `openagent/routes/fine_tuning_routes.py` declared seven `/api/fine-tuning/*` routes and was referenced nowhere — not in `DOMAIN_ROUTE_MODULES`, not imported by the runtime, and not called by either frontend. It was left behind when `fine_tuning_service.py` was removed; the live surface is `finetune_routes.py` serving `/api/finetune/*`. Removed.
- `openagent/routes/voice_websocket_routes.py` declared six `/api/voice/stream/continuous*` routes and was likewise imported nowhere. The wired, scope-mapped voice surface is `voice_routes.py` (`POST /api/voice/stream/continuous` plus `/api/voice/websocket/create|chunk|finish`) over `VoiceWebSocketStreamManager`; the removed module was a parallel declaration of the same capability under paths nothing dispatched. Removed. The continuous socket transport remains the documented optional decision under L12 — deleting an unreachable duplicate declaration removes no capability.

Both removals left the registry at 783/54 before the legacy-attribution registration, which confirms neither module contributed a reachable route. `tests/test_legacy_attribution_registration.py` — **7 passed, 5 subtests** — pins the module into the domain list and the contract, pins the read and write scopes, and asserts as a registry-wide invariant that the only ungated registered routes are those four public paths, so a future module that is dispatched without being registered fails this test rather than going unnoticed.

- `cd frontend-second; npx tsc --noEmit` — **clean**.
- `cd frontend-second; npx vitest run` — **205 passed / 26 files**, exit 0.
- `cd frontend-second; npm run test:e2e -- --workers=2 --retries=0` — **269 passed, exit 0** (6.1m).
- `cd frontend-second; npm run test:real-python` — **51 workflows / 534 API responses / zero Demo fixture requests, overall PASS, exit 0**.

One caveat is recorded rather than hidden: an earlier Playwright run of the same source reported **268 passed, 1 failed** on `tests/e2e/home-chat-attachment.spec.ts` at 1280×800 light, a 5-second `toBeVisible` timeout. That run was started while the 2,900-test Python suite was executing on the same machine. The spec passed 5/5 on its own and the full suite then passed 269/269 uncontended, so the failure was CPU contention from running the two gates together, not a defect in the attachment hand-off. The uncontended run is the one recorded above.

**Refusal statuses and path matching (2026-11-02):** four services report a record belonging to another project or owner as an `error` key rather than by raising, and the route layer flattened all of them. A caller — and the screen — could not tell "your request was malformed" from "this is not yours" from "there is no such record".

- `/api/g25/memory/history` and `/api/g25/memory/store` answered a refusal with **HTTP 200 and `success: true`**, the reason buried in `data.error`, so a denied read was announced as an operation the backend had completed. Now 403 for another project or owner, 404 for an unknown session.
- Every refusal in `voice_conversation_routes.py` and `model_serve_routes.py` was a blanket **400**. Now 403 for a scope refusal and 404 for an unknown record, with the non-ownership cases deliberately left at 400: a missing model file (the session exists and is the caller's; the host is not ready for it), an undownloaded model (the caller may make the request once it is downloaded, which `requires_download` says), and a provider failure such as a transcription engine being absent.
- `model_serve_routes.py` matched session reads with `path.startswith(...) and path.endswith("")`. The second test is true for every path, so it filtered nothing, and a read of a sub-path such as `/api/model-serve/sessions/{id}/start` was answered as a lookup of the id `"{id}/start"` instead of falling through as an unknown route. Both occurrences now require a bare id. `voice_conversation_routes.py` had the same unbounded prefix match and now does too.

`tests/test_route_refusal_statuses.py` — **11 passed, 4 subtests** — pins each mapping in both directions and asserts the four services still use the wording the mappers key on, so renaming a refusal message fails a test rather than silently turning a 403 back into a 400. The smoke now asserts these statuses over real HTTP: reading a conversation under another project is 404 while responding into it is 403 (the read filters by project in its lookup and cannot find it; respond finds it and then refuses the scope), and starting another project's serving session is 403 — **51 workflows / 542 API responses / zero Demo fixture requests, exit 0**.

Two of my own expectations were wrong before the code was: I first asserted 404 for the respond and start probes. The services look the record up by id and then check scope, so 403 is the correct and more precise answer, and the assertions were corrected to it rather than the behaviour being changed to match the guess.

**Production Readiness screen (2026-11-03):** this is a Live destination whose own definition promises "real-time status of all production-ready features including live transcription, reminder delivery, training jobs, and model sessions". Three of its four tabs invented everything they displayed, which is the plainest violation of this audit's standard that no simulated record may appear as Live data.

What was fabricated, all of it hardcoded or random rather than read from any route:

- **Voice STT** — a Start/Stop recorder that touched no microphone, a hardcoded `Sample transcription: "This is a test voice message with real-time STT"`, a confidence from `Math.random()`, twenty waveform bars from `Math.random()`, a fixed `24ms` latency, a subtitle reading "WebSocket streaming with real STT", three permanent green ticks asserting that Faster Whisper, a live socket connection and audio buffering were all running, and two invented sessions with invented frame counts and "2 min ago" start times.
- **Auto Scheduling** — three invented reminders, "12 delivered today", "3 pending", a "60s check interval", and three permanent green indicators for a background worker, calendar integration and an integration hub.
- **Fine-tuning** — three invented jobs with progress bars and ETAs, "3 active jobs", a "2.4GB" checkpoint size, an "FP32" precision, "1 validated", and three green ticks for FP32, BF16 and worker-failure tracking.
- **Ultra Model** — read its two endpoints through a bare `fetch('/api/ultra-model/status')` with no credential, so it was answered 401 on any authenticated install; and **on failure it substituted invented sessions** (`Llama-7B`, `ready`, `4h 32m`) into the Live screen. It also showed `Q4_K_M`, `GGUF` and `llama-server` as fallbacks whenever the backend reported no format or serve type.
- **Tab badges** read 2, 3, 3, 2 regardless of what any service held, so every tab advertised a count of records that did not exist.

What it does now, with no stand-in anywhere: Voice STT reads `/api/voice/status` for the real session, turn and translation counts and the real per-engine availability, saying why an engine is unavailable instead of showing a tick, and stating plainly that recording runs over buffered HTTP from the Voice screen and that there is no accepted continuous socket transport. Auto Scheduling reads `/api/calendar/reminders` and counts delivered against not delivered from the `delivered` column the calendar system records. Fine-tuning reads `/api/finetune/jobs` and counts by real status, drawing a progress bar only where progress is reported and otherwise saying "Progress not reported". Ultra Model reads both endpoints through the authenticated client and, when a read fails, shows the connection message and nothing else. Figures no route reports — checkpoint size, precision, worker health — are not displayed at all, with a line saying where that state actually lives rather than a removed card leaving a silent gap.

`frontend-second/tests/production-readiness-truthful.test.tsx` — **8 passed** — holds each tab to the backend's payload and asserts the absence of each former fabrication by name, including that the Ultra Model reads carry the shared client's headers (a bare `fetch` would not) and that a failed read leaves no invented session behind.

Clearing `test-results` and running the suite uncontended gave 269/269; the spec in question also passes on its own. Neither failure was a product assertion, and neither number is presented above as the result.

## Small local model verification — 26 October 2026

Owner approved the smallest appropriate model to save time. Verification uses a **4,656-parameter Llama architecture initialized locally**, not pretrained weights: one layer, hidden16, vocabulary64, one epoch, two examples, rank2, sequence length8 and one CPU thread. **No model weights were downloaded.** This verifies the implemented worker's numerical update/checkpoint path, not all model families/sizes, language quality, standard Llama task-loss training or an arbitrary large-model claim.

- Existing `.accelerator-venv/Scripts/python.exe` has PyTorch2.14.1+cpu, safetensors and transformers. Its missing web/security dependencies were installed within that workspace environment using the project ranges (cryptography49.0.0, argon2-cffi25.1.0, Pillow12.3.0). The normal `.venv` and installed application were not switched or upgraded.
- Actual worker runs passed for **FP32 and BF16**:4 adapter modules updated,1 real epoch checkpoint persisted, unchanged base-weight hashes, and completed job/checkpoint reload verified. Reproduce with `.accelerator-venv\Scripts\python.exe tests/support/tiny_finetune_verification.py --dtype float32` or `--dtype bfloat16`. [FP32 log](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-07-float32-final.log), [BF16 log](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-07-bfloat16-final.log). Retained test-only base/config/adapter artifacts: [FP32](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-float32-artifacts/verification-origin.json), [BF16](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-bfloat16-artifacts/verification-origin.json).
- Actual React-to-Python training start/completion/progress/checkpoint bytes/base hash and reload passed at both desktop sizes/themes: **4/4**, [tiny-03-browser.log](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-03-browser.log), [browser source](tests/e2e/tiny-finetune-live-python.spec.ts). This uses a real PyTorch worker and SQLite, with no worker/model-response mocks. The fixture is local auth-disabled; permission boundaries remain separate regressions.
- Source fixes from this verification: completed/failed/cancelled jobs remain selectable for inspection; status includes actual adapter ID/error; UI does not claim adapter registration without an ID; missing checkpoint/adapter persistence now fails the job rather than silently completing it; CPU math converts FP16/BF16 layers to FP32 while leaving files frozen; integer/quantized targets fail clearly instead of being treated as dequantized weights; `.bin` loading uses `weights_only=True`.
- **Superseded 1 November 2026:** the normal backend now has PyTorch 2.15.0+cpu in `.venv`, product status reports `cpu_training.torch_installed=true` there, and 28 fine-tuning tests (`tests.test_c1_real_model_training`, `tests.test_finetune_routes`) pass under that interpreter, so Live Start is no longer disabled on this host. The algorithm remains `approximate-layer-proxy-lora`; this is a runtime-availability fact and still certifies no pretrained model or language quality. The standalone `tests/support/tiny_finetune_verification.py` fixture additionally needs `transformers` and `safetensors`, which only `.accelerator-venv` has, so that richer check still runs there. Original finding: the normal backend lacks PyTorch. Status now exposes `cpu_training.torch_installed`; Live Start is disabled with an honest message when false. Verification under the ML Python does **not** establish that the currently installed normal backend can train without a compatible ML runtime.
- Persistence fault injection and applicable Python regressions: **33 passed**, [tiny-06-python-final.log](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-06-python-final.log). These fault-injection tests are not themselves genuine training evidence; the direct and browser runs above are.
- **Ultra Model launch remains BLOCKED-ENV:** actual capability lookup reports `llama_server.available=false`, path empty; [readiness evidence](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/tiny-06-ultra-readiness.json). Its runner requires a real llama-server executable and compatible GGUF. The tiny safetensors training fixture cannot substitute for that format/runtime. No fake server, fabricated GGUF, invented model answer or unsafe launch was used.
- Final checks after the runtime-availability UI and dtype changes are pending the focused verification below. Earlier265/203/293 counts belong to the preceding source pass, not a newly claimed269-test run.

Actual preset PUT/DELETE and existing Knowledge PUT disconnect with NameError. The Knowledge service mutation executes before the dispatcher crash. Underlying preset PUT also passes unsupported `preset_id` and DELETE refers to absent `delete_preset`. [monitor-02-new-routes.json](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/monitor-02-new-routes.json). |
| G25 / degraded Agent result (`1156696`) | Source now renders degraded/fallback status. | The UI inspects backend metadata and labels the response. Behavioral regression for degraded versus normal/completed/cancelled output remains pending. |

Latest affected Python selection: `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest tests.test_c4_agent_event_stream tests.test_personal_os tests.test_model_cookbook tests.test_knowledge_routes tests.test_semantic_routes -v` — **38 run, 36 passed, 2 errors** (foreign-owner C4 assertion and Knowledge HTTP update). Existing local Personal OS create still returns 201. No full suite, provider run, production build or visual sweep was repeated during this monitor cycle. New route inventory counts supersede initial 713-entry figures after refresh; original test logs remain intact.

The two fresh Python selections total **223 tests: 222 passed and 1 failed**, with no skipped tests reported. Passing C1/C3/C5 names are not treated as training, provider or WebSocket proof: their bodies mock the worker/providers/Voice service or manually inject progress/checkpoints. C2 verifies explicit scheduling and delivery methods/HTTP calls, not a background delivery loop. C4 primarily exercises manager calls, not the HTTP SSE transport.

### Permission mapping and handler review

- The current mapper covers **779/783** declared method/path entries. Four unmapped entries are `GET /api/health` and the auth register/login/refresh POSTs. Health is public; auth handlers apply installation bootstrap/owner policy or validate supplied credentials. These four are **not** a finding of unprotected private routes. The current focused auth and Agent history selection passed 20 tests. A scope mapping still does not prove handler-internal ownership or approval checks.
- Every declared entry appears below with its module and located dispatcher symbol. This is declaration and source-location coverage; an individual branch is runtime-verified only where a dated test above explicitly says so. The previously reported Voice continuous dispatcher 404 was fixed in the dated October check; that does not establish a WebSocket upgrade or live microphone streaming.
- Current bounded source counts are **413** frontend-second candidates from **17** production TS/JS files and **243** original frontend candidates from **40** files. **370** declarations have no bounded frontend-second literal. Dynamic construction and unmounted components can affect these counts. These are **not 413 wired integrations or 370 missing features**.
- The older reverse scan found **436 literal URL occurrences** and **16 unmatched candidates** on its October source snapshot, archived in [frontend-unmatched-route-candidates.json](.qa/gap-audit-20261026/frontend-unmatched-route-candidates.json). It is **not a current-tree count**. A fresh reverse scan is needed before inferring additional missing Live endpoints from unmatched literals.
- Adaptive Engine's 29 declarations are inventoried for complete registry accounting and excluded from requested capability closure. No team-account, remote-access or deployment architecture work was performed.

## Direction C — existing wiring requiring final verification

The older seven-point list here described fixed compiler/dispatcher defects and an obsolete 199/241/45 test baseline as if they were current. The **2 November one-pass closure board (L01–L13)** is now the authoritative action order. For existing wired actions, the remaining verification rule is: activate the React control, capture the actual Python method/path/status/schema, verify a fresh read or persisted reload, exercise a denied scope or foreign project where relevant, and compare the resulting approved desktop state. Static strings, intercepted 503s and Demo fixtures are separate evidence categories.

Current priority is the final full Python result, autonomous CodeQ edit-run result, same-revision frontend/real-Python regressions, Windows installed launch, then equivalent-state visual/control acceptance. Polling, provider/hardware limitations and restricted legacy rows retain the truthful dispositions in L09–L13. No global project selector, remote/team architecture or automatic legacy-owner attribution is part of this local release.

## Current disposition and next verification

- **Current authoritative action list:** L01–L13 above, plus the detailed G and B rows. The 788-route registry, scope mapper and 32 navigation destinations were re-read on 2 November. Counts are declaration/source evidence, not a functional completion percentage.
- **Previously reported faults now closed at their tested boundary:** original TypeScript diagnostics, Agent/Workflow SSE object framing, Voice dispatcher 404, agent cancellation smoke fixture, CodeQ empty edit values/numbered-context patch failures, and the Windows managed-preview log-release race. Do not reopen them without a reproduced regression.
- **Open release checks:** final full Python discovery; a genuinely completed CodeQ autonomous edit or truthful supported-model restriction; same-revision frontend and React-to-Python regressions; installed desktop launch; and equivalent-state visual/control acceptance. The detailed acceptance commands and decisions are L01–L06.
- **Optional/restricted:** provider/hardware operations, legacy ownerless attribution, new privileged UI placement and unsupported event transports are L07–L13. Keep truthful Live unavailable states and local Demo isolation. No remote/team architecture, destructive migration or uncontrolled external side effect is authorized by this audit.
- **Release status:** OPEN. This file gives a bounded stop condition; it does not claim that an audit alone ships the application.

## Latest source-backed status — 2026-10-26

### Knowledge semantic collection archive/restore — implemented in this update

The semantic collection lifecycle now supports reversible archive and restore in Python and React. `POST /api/semantic/collections/{collection_name}/archive` uses the existing `semantic:write` permission; `archived` must be a boolean and the operation is project-scoped. Normal listings hide archived entries; `include_archived=true` includes them. Migration 33 adds archive metadata without deleting collection/vector rows. Restore preserves the original collection ID and vector records. Demo mode mirrors this operation only in its local fixture store. Verification on 2026-10-26: focused Python service/route/migration **45 tests passed**; Knowledge Demo E2E **1/1 passed**, including archive/restore persistence and zero `/api/` requests; isolated real React-to-Python Knowledge smoke **4 workflows passed** with authentication enforced and external providers disabled, including archived/active list reads and restore identity preservation. TypeScript check and isolated production build passed.

This section supersedes older claims below where the same capability appears. Historical audit entries are retained for provenance and are not current status.

- **Knowledge semantic collections:** collection list, rename, reversible archive/restore and delete use the same `/api/semantic/collections` resource. Rename/archive/restore preserve collection identity and vectors; delete removes collection vectors. Demo mutations stay local. Archive/restore now has explicit Demo E2E and actual React-to-Python persistence coverage (details in the dated update above).
- **Agent run events:** the React Agents view uses authenticated cursor-based incremental polling of `/api/agents/jobs/{id}/events`. This is resumable polling, not server-push SSE. No new route was added.
- **Communications permanent delete:** React confirmation calls `DELETE /api/communication/messages/{id}?project=default`; backend filters by project and authenticated owner. Current targeted actual React-to-Python smoke passes permanent deletion and verifies absence on a fresh inbox read. No message is sent.
- **Calendar occurrence overrides:** the event detail exposes “Edit this occurrence”; React persists a single occurrence with `POST /api/calendar/events/{id}/override`, while series edit/delete remain separate. Selected Calendar smoke verifies the override and fresh-read persistence.
- **Calendar provider lifecycle:** provider register, re-authenticate and remove controls use existing local provider contracts. Selected Calendar smoke verifies them with synthetic metadata and no external provider call.
- **Calendar reminders:** persisted preferences control manual delivery, and runtime also starts a local autonomous worker that passes those preferences. Source and helper tests exist; complete worker opt-out/restart/dedup verification and any external delivery remain unverified.
- **Personal Assistant activity:** Live Today reads `GET /api/assistant/activity?project=default&limit=20`, guarded by `assistant:read`. The service requires an explicit project and filters to task/goal events only, avoiding the broad admin-only Security audit route. An isolated React-to-Python smoke passed task create/update/complete/delete, filtered activity rendering, goal create/update/delete and persistence with authentication enforced.
- **Voice incremental capture:** the existing Live React capture opens `/api/voice/stream/start`, uploads ordered `/chunk` requests and calls `/finish`, with batch-upload fallback. This is chunked HTTP delivery, not a persistent browser socket. Actual device/provider audio quality remains environment-dependent.
- **Workspace archive/restore:** Files UI exposes archive and restore, and the prior isolated smoke verifies both across a fresh Python read. This is a soft archive; delete remains separate.
- **Research legacy ownership:** enforced-auth mode deliberately withholds ownerless legacy sessions (404); there is no silent ownership attribution. Local unauthenticated single-user compatibility is separate.
- **Fine-tuning Live worker controls:** the Live Jobs panel now only starts queued jobs, allows a selected running job to be monitored, exposes cooperative cancellation after a genuine backend status is observed, and reports polling failures instead of silently stopping. Its copy now identifies the worker's approximate tokenization/update method and does not promise arbitrary model compatibility. This is UI correctness, not evidence of model-quality or production model-family compatibility.

Focused verification for this update: Assistant Python tests **11/11**; Vitest backend-wiring tests **51/51** including queued-only launch, backend polling, running cancellation and terminal state; TypeScript check passed. Selected React-to-Python smokes previously passed for Personal Assistant **3 workflows**, Calendar **5 workflows**, Communications **3 workflows**, and Knowledge **4 workflows**. The latest complete isolated React-to-Python smoke passed **45 workflows / 467 API responses**, with authentication enforced, external providers disabled, and zero Demo fixture requests. This is broad selected integration coverage, not verification of every route or external service.

### Latest verification after the current source changes — 2026-10-25

- `cd frontend-second; npm run check` — passed.
- `cd frontend-second; npm test -- --reporter=dot --maxWorkers=1` — **199 passed across 25 files**.
- `cd frontend-second; npm run test:e2e -- --workers=1 --retries=0` — **241 passed**. The immediately preceding run exposed four stale Live Personal Assistant assertions still targeting `/api/audit`; the test now targets `/api/assistant/activity?project=default&limit=20`, and the full suite was rerun to exit code 0.
- `cd frontend-second; npm run test:real-python` — **45 workflows passed, 467 API responses**, actual isolated Python HTTP server and React browser, auth enforced, external providers disabled, zero Demo fixture requests. Fine-tuning exercised queued record cancellation, not model-training completion or checkpoint generation.

### Earlier pass notes (historical; superseded by latest status above)

The following bullets preserve the prior pass's notes. Several are stale and superseded by the current status at the top of this document; consult that status before acting on them.

- **Knowledge collection rename/delete (superseded endpoint note):** controls now call `/api/semantic/collections`; the displayed records belong to that store. The prior `/api/knowledge/collections` note below referred to a different resource.

- **Agent incremental event stream** (`OperationsScreens.tsx`): `jobEventsQuery` now uses cursor-based incremental polling. Each refetch fetches only events after the last `next` cursor via `?after=…`, accumulating events in a ref rather than re-fetching the full list. The 1500 ms poll interval and job-status gate are unchanged.

- **Communications permanent delete** — already wired (`AdvancedScreen.tsx:1802`, `DELETE /api/communication/messages/{id}`). No change needed.

- **Calendar reminder delivery** (`WorkspaceScreens.tsx`): `deliverRemindersMutation` added, calling `POST /api/calendar/reminders/deliver`. A "Deliver" button is added to the Reminders section head; it is disabled in demo mode and while pending.

- **Security Audit live feed** — already wired. `RouteContent` renders the audit event list for the security Audit tab via the existing endpoint map (`{ events: "/api/audit?project=default&limit=100" }`) and specialized `SecurityAuditTab` display. No change needed; `LiveData` correctly excludes security to avoid duplication.

- **Personal Assistant live feed** — already wired in a prior pass (see table row above). No change needed.

- **Research legacy sessions** (`WorkspaceScreens.tsx`): `includeUnowned` state and query parameter `&include_unowned=true` added. An "Include unowned" checkbox appears in the session selector row alongside the existing "Include archived" checkbox. This passes the parameter to the backend; whether the backend honours it depends on server-side implementation.

Historical unresolved-item list (superseded): occurrence editing and chunked Voice upload are implemented and selected-smoke verified. Current unresolved items include real Fine-tuning worker/checkpoint verification, agent server-push SSE (cursor polling exists), any browser-socket Voice transport beyond ordered HTTP chunks, unattended notification scheduling/external delivery, and the per-screen integration coverage gaps described below. There is no mandatory global project selector in the approved local single-owner architecture.

This is a source-and-test check of the claims in the user-provided table, not a declaration that every listed workflow is release-complete. Rows below distinguish a route/service existing from an end-to-end React workflow or unattended production behavior being verified.

| Claim | Current finding | Evidence / limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Media owner isolation | **Verified for authenticated owner-scoped media reads and mutations; local single-user mode intentionally has no account owner filter.** | Media asset listing, status, analysis, playback and deletion apply the authenticated owner. Chat media lookup also carries the chat owner. Added HTTP regression: authenticated Alice can create/play her TTS asset; Bob receives 404. `tests.test_owner_scope`, `tests.test_media_routes`, `tests.test_media_voice`, `tests.test_voice`; actual React/Python smoke covers media and voice. |
| Knowledge owner isolation | **Verified at handler/service/query level for owner-scoped collection/document/search paths.** | Migration 32 and `vector_store.py` owner predicates; Knowledge handlers pass the request owner. Enforced-mode legacy ownerless data is not automatically attributed. Focused HTTP and service tests plus React/Python smoke cover the supported Knowledge operations. |
| Fine-tuning CPU worker | **Worker implementation exists and unit-tested; real model-training completion is not verified.** | `run_job_cpu_streaming()` and the CPU engine emit worker callbacks. React/Python smoke verifies safe queued-job cancellation only; it does not run a real training job or assert a genuine checkpoint was produced. Demo previews/checkpoints remain simulated and must not be represented as Live results. |
| Fine-tuning running-job cancellation | **Implemented as cooperative cancellation.** | A running job enters `cancelling`; the worker checks cancellation and finalizes it, preventing late progress/completion from overwriting cancellation. Unit tests cover cancellation races. The real integration smoke does not run a long training job. |
| Fine-tuning checkpoint manifest | **Routes/service and React read mapping exist; actual training-generated checkpoint persistence is unverified.** | `GET /api/finetune/checkpoints` and `GET /api/finetune/checkpoints/{checkpoint_id}` are declared and implemented. A manifest contract is not evidence that a real training run generated checkpoints. |
| Assistant activity / Security audit | **Assistant feed uses dedicated `assistant:read`; Security audit remains admin-only.** | `GET /api/assistant/activity` requires explicit project scope and filters to task/goal audit actions. Real React-to-Python smoke verified the task lifecycle feed. Security remains `GET /api/audit?project=default&limit=100` under `security:admin`. |
| Research legacy attribution | **The table's unqualified claim is inaccurate.** | Enforced-auth tests show ownerless legacy sessions are excluded and return 404 for authenticated users. Local single-user mode can retain access to local legacy history. No silent ownership migration occurs. See `tests.test_research_owner_access`. |
| Workspace archive/restore | **React Live flow verified against isolated Python.** | Files UI archives/restores a disposable workspace item; a fresh authenticated list with `include_archived=true` and then the active list verified both state transitions in the real smoke. |
| Knowledge semantic collection lifecycle | **Demo and Live verified, including archive/restore.** | `/api/semantic/collections` list/create, `/rename`, `/archive`, and DELETE use the semantic collection store. Actual React→Python smoke verified stable ID/vector retention for rename/archive/restore, active versus archived filtering and delete persistence. This is distinct from indexed Knowledge documents. |
| Calendar occurrence overrides | **React flow verified against isolated Python.** | `create_event_override()` and `POST /api/calendar/events/{id}/override` persist one occurrence; smoke verified changed title/location/time and unchanged series title on a fresh read. |

No external delivery provider is claimed. |
| Python tests | **The cited 76-test result is historical, not the current verification count.** | Current targeted regression selection: **188 passed, 7 skipped** across media/owner, Knowledge, Fine-tuning, workspace, Calendar, Research, Security, Agents and Communications (2026-10-25). |
