## Batch 2B revised solo architecture — implementation verification (2026-10-19)

The approved product model is one owner per installation, with local loopback desktop use and opt-in personal remote access. This section supersedes the earlier multi-user security blockers for the **`personal_remote` profile only**. It does not certify the legacy `team` or `enterprise` modes.

- **Verified in source and isolated HTTP tests:** personal remote requires `OPENAGENT_AUTH_ENFORCED=1`, loopback Python binding, HTTPS-terminated proxy mode and an explicit allowed-host list. Remote requests are rejected for an unapproved Host, non-HTTPS forwarded protocol, or unapproved Origin. The public route allowlist is limited to health, login, refresh and bootstrap registration. Every other declared private route has a scope mapping; unmapped private paths fail closed. Owner identity is checked against the only active installation owner. A second account or ambiguous legacy account store prevents personal-remote startup without modifying it.
- **Research, Knowledge and Media:** all private HTTP/SSE/API requests pass the personal-remote identity and scope gate before handlers execute. In the real React→Python smoke, owner operations succeeded and a bearer credential issued by a separate disposable installation received HTTP 401 for Research, Knowledge and Media. Server-path media ingestion is rejected in personal remote; byte uploads remain available. Existing local records are retained; no per-record owner migration was performed.
- **Owner sign-in:** Settings → Advanced now supports owner username/password sign-in, optional authenticator code, current-session identification, refresh and sign-out. The access token remains in tab-scoped session storage. This path was exercised through the React browser against the disposable Python server; logout cleared the tab token and a subsequent private `/api/auth/me` returned 401.
- **Smoke harness:** any recorded `BLOCKED` or `FAIL` result now causes a non-zero process exit. The latest actual run completed with 18/18 workflows passing, 132 API responses, zero blocked workflows, and zero Demo-fixture requests.
- **Known boundary:** the remote request boundary was verified with the actual loopback Python HTTP server and controlled HTTPS-proxy Host/forwarding headers. No TLS reverse proxy, VPN, Internet exposure, or second physical remote device was deployed or tested. Do not treat test headers as proof of a production network deployment.
- **Legacy team/enterprise:** the Batch 2A same-project, cross-account Research/Knowledge/Media issues are still not fixed for legacy multi-user modes. Such modes are outside the approved product architecture and remain unsupported/unverified. Do not enable them as a substitute for personal remote.
- **Remaining product gaps:** production remote proxy packaging, first-run operator/bootstrap guidance and a real private-network multi-device walkthrough still need deployment verification. Remaining Batch 2C advertised-workflow gaps are tracked below and are not closed by route declarations or this smoke alone.

## Batch 2A current integration result — 2026-10-19 (supersedes the earlier Batch 1-only summary below)

- **Implemented existing-contract wiring and verification:** `frontend-second` now has real local-Python smoke coverage for Chat SSE success/failure/cancel; CodeQ project/run/SSE cursor reconnect/cancel/fail/resume; Research session/report/checkpoints/events/cancel; Agent checked runs/history/approval; limited Models, Files and Calendar reads; Files document import/read/download and media upload; Knowledge ingestion and multipart upload; Communications reply draft/archive without send; and Calendar create/edit/delete/read. The full smoke used the actual React app, authenticated local Python HTTP server, disposable SQLite/runtime directory, two synthetic users, local deterministic provider, and no external providers. It made 0 Demo-fixture requests.
- **SECURITY BLOCKER — Research:** with enforced authentication, a second synthetic user with only `knowledge:read` could list the first user's Research session and retrieve its report by ID. Current `openagent/routes/runtime.py` Research handlers call the lifecycle by project/session ID without binding the authenticated owner. The integration smoke records this as `BLOCKED`; do not treat Research live multi-user access as safe. Requires an approved owner/project authorization design before this contract is used for multi-user deployments.
- **SECURITY BLOCKER — Knowledge:** with enforced authentication, a second synthetic user with `knowledge:read` could list the first user's indexed Knowledge source under the same project name. Current Knowledge storage/API is project-name scoped without authenticated owner binding. The smoke records this as `BLOCKED`; requires an approved isolation design. No Python authorization behavior was changed in Batch 2A.
- **SECURITY BLOCKER — uploaded media:** with enforced authentication, a second synthetic user with `media:read` could list the first user's uploaded file under the same project name. `GET /api/media/assets` and media persistence currently filter by project string but not authenticated owner. The smoke records this as `BLOCKED`; requires an approved owner-binding design. No Python authorization behavior was changed in Batch 2A.
- **Files isolation:** verified document import and content read in the owner session; a second authenticated user received 404 for the same document ID. Local download was triggered from the actual React UI.
- **Other verified workflows:** Agents history requires explicit `agents:read`; a run-only token can execute but cannot read history, unscoped/unrelated tokens are denied, cross-owner history returns non-disclosing 404, and the React approval request was rejected through an admin-scoped operation. Communications reply creates a saved draft and archive updates the existing record; no send call occurred. Calendar event create/edit/delete persisted to the existing read route.
- **No scope expansion:** no new API route or backend capability was added. Existing API adapters, SSE auth/cursors and Demo/Live boundaries are retained. React live Research currently remains source-wired to the existing contract, but the tested cross-user leak is a release blocker; do not interpret source wiring or single-user success as authorization safety.
- **Route inventory:** refreshed declarations contain **655 method/path entries across 49 groups**. The table below is regenerated from the current route modules; literal source hits remain candidates, not proof of runtime wiring.
- **Latest exact commands/results:** see `BATCH2A_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md`. On the final source revision, `npm run check` passed; Vitest passed **152/152 across 22 files**; desktop Playwright passed **193/193**; isolated production build passed; production Live preview regression passed with 12 safely stubbed 503 API responses, zero demo fixture requests and zero page errors; targeted Python regressions passed **61 tests**. The final real React-to-Python smoke exited 0 with 129 API responses and zero Demo fixture requests. It verified the named workflows while explicitly blocking three cross-user isolation checks (Research, Knowledge, uploaded media); those are release/security blockers, not successful workflows. No production Python-provider integration was claimed.

## Batch 1 security and React-to-Python verification — 2026-10-19 (historical; superseded by the current Batch 2A section above)

- **Agent history security fix implemented:** `GET /api/agent-runs` is declared in the route registry and requires the new `agents:read` scope under enforced authentication. The scope is not included in the existing session/default scope set; `agents:run` alone is insufficient. The handler requires a non-empty project, ignores caller-supplied `owner`, and returns the same 404 for an unknown or non-owned project. Pagination is bounded and owner-filtered.
- **Ownership/persistence:** migration 24 adds `agent_run_projects` and a nullable-compatible `agent_runs.owner` column; migration 25 adds the owner/project history index. The authenticated principal ID is taken from the verified request context, never from the submitted body/query. The first authorized agent-run creation binds an unbound project namespace to that principal; the binding cannot be reassigned. The run recording path persists that principal. Existing rows with no proven owner remain ownerless and return 404 in enforced mode; no automatic account attribution is performed. Local single-user mode remains available when enforcement is off.
- **Security evidence:** `tests.test_agent_run_access` and the related auth, migration and route-contract checks passed **42/42** after the latest source edit. Coverage includes anonymous, invalid/expired bearer, run-only and unrelated scopes, missing/empty project, unknown/forged/cross-owner project, legacy ownerless rows, pagination, default session scope, project binding and normal agent execution. A separate local HTTP integration also confirmed a different authenticated user receives 404 for the same project while the authorized owner sees its run.
- **React:** after the backend authorization tests passed, `frontend-second/src/screens/OperationsScreens.tsx::AgentsScreen` enabled the existing Live history query against `/api/agent-runs?project=…&limit=50`; Demo history still uses its isolated provider. A 404 is rendered as an empty history to avoid disclosing project existence. The UI never sends an owner selector.
- **Real local integration (not a mock HTTP response):** `npm run test:real-python` starts the actual Python HTTP server with auth enforced, temporary SQLite/runtime storage, two synthetic users, a disposable CodeQ repository, and a deterministic in-process local model gateway. The actual React app runs through Vite's proxy. Passed workflows: Chat SSE + persisted user/assistant messages; Chat provider error; Chat cancellation; CodeQ create/run, authenticated event SSE/cursor, React cancellation and persisted cancellation, controlled failure and React resume; Research session/report/checkpoint, authenticated React event stream and rendered queued/planning event, React cancellation and persisted cancelled status/checkpoint; authorized Agent history and denied cross-owner read; Models, Files and Calendar basic reads. No Demo fixture requests or external provider calls. This is isolated integration evidence, not a production-service/provider test.
- **Remaining integration coverage:** CodeQ stream reconnection after browser/network loss remains unverified. Remaining rows in Direction A/C stay open until separately verified. This pass added only the scoped stored-media deletion operation described above; no unrelated backend capability was added.
- **Current package checks:** `npm run check` passed; frontend unit suite passed **151/151 tests in 22 files**; relevant Python regressions passed **74/74**, including the final CodeQ project-namespace guard and Agent-history authorization tests. Desktop Playwright passed **193/193 in 6.2 minutes** using the runner-owned Vite server; the Chat pending-indicator focused matrix passed 4/4 and the project-chooser matched-state comparison passed 1/1. The real React/Python smoke passed all 9 workflows (74 API responses, zero Demo fixture requests, external providers disabled), including CodeQ resume and Research cancellation. Isolated production build passed to `frontend-second/dist`; `../web` remained unchanged and `public/demo` fixtures were excluded. Production preview stale-Demo regression passed with 12 offline 503 stubs, zero Demo fixture requests and zero browser errors. Root-cause and exact command evidence is in `BATCH1_IMPLEMENTATION_RESULTS.md` and `PLAYWRIGHT_BATCH1_FINAL.log`. Older verification sections below are historical and are not current Batch 1 results.

## Latest security authorization correction — 2026-10-18

- `/api/audit` previously had no enforced-auth scope and, without a `project`
  query, returned audit records across projects. `required_scope_for_path()` now
  assigns this exact legacy route `security:admin`; ordinary `security:read`
  tokens are denied because this response can be global. The request-level
  regression confirms anonymous access is 401, `security:read` is 403, and a
  `security:admin` token can read the global feed. Focused auth/security tests
  pass 9 cases. This closes the unauthenticated route exposure only; it does
  not add per-project membership semantics, change local single-user mode, or
  verify a deployed multi-user configuration.
- Historical note: the 2026-10-18 Live Security Audit implementation withheld
  the event list while project scoping was unresolved. It is superseded by the
  2026-10-24 implementation below, which uses the existing route with an
  explicit default-project filter and keeps the `security:admin` permission.

It renders explicit loading/error/empty states and does not manufacture progress. Verified route handlers are in `openagent/routes/adaptive_engine_routes.py` (`GET /api/adaptive-engine/status`, `/hardware`, `/orchestrations`, `/plans`) plus existing `/api/compute/status` and `/api/adaptive-engine/direct/status`; payload fields were checked against `openagent/adaptive_engine/runtime.py` and `hardware.py`. Personal Assistant Live Today separately requests `/api/audit?project=default&scope=assistant&limit=20` through the existing endpoint map; it is not Demo-only. Because the backend currently maps the route to `security:admin`, this activity is available only when the local owner credential has that permission; permission failure is shown rather than replaced with sample activity. The focused React regression verifies the exact scoped query and rendered event. Existing `POST /api/security/audit-chain/verify` remains `security:admin`; `GET /api/security/status` remains `security:read`. ## Production stale-Demo safety regression — 2026-10-17

- Fixed the same-origin upgrade case where production state was Live but a persisted development Demo flag caused the API guard to block calls. `src/state.ts::readDataMode()` clears the stale key where possible; `src/api/client.ts::assertLiveRequestsAllowed()` honors production's effective Live-only state while retaining an in-memory Demo block and development persisted-Demo fail-closed behavior.
- `requestHeaders()` now preserves caller-supplied `Accept`, so SSE POST retains `text/event-stream`. Existing SSE GET and POST auth/event parsing tests cover cursor handling.
- Final regression: production preview with stale Demo key removed it and made 3 API calls to offline 503 stubs, with no `demo_mode_request_blocked`, no synthetic trace/playback, and zero page errors. This confirms request attempt only, not Python integration.
- Full final run: TypeScript passed; unit 140/140; Playwright 57/57; isolated build passed; production stale-key preview regression passed. Exact commands/results are in `LIVING_UI_TEST_RESULTS.md`.
- No backend source or API capability changed; desktop visual evidence was not recaptured because this patch changes only state initialization/request guarding and SSE header preservation.

The additional all-workspace functional smoke test visited all 23 screens, activated every visible tab in demo mode, and recorded zero page errors, failed requests, or `/api/` requests. No Python backend suite or live UI-to-Python integration test was run; backend UI tests use mocked responses and do not prove a live deployment.

The 152 named interaction pairs are representative and do not cover every dialog, notification, form, detail view, nested menu, loading/empty/error state, or every execution failure/cancellation/approval state. The current interaction catalog marks uncovered states as unverified. Personal Assistant goal creation/editing and other covered local actions use deterministic fixtures. Workspace-wide visual and interaction gaps remain; acceptance is **FAIL / OPEN** and Milestone A is not complete.

This table maps each screen to its component module and the route families used in that module. Several files contain multiple screen components, so a module-level route family does not by itself prove that every listed route belongs to that one screen. The complete inventory below maps each registered method/path row to exact source files that contain its path pattern. All of those mappings are static candidates; the current test run did not exercise them all against Python.

Adapter map: app shell/data mode `src/App.tsx`, `src/state.ts`; API/auth/error adapter `src/api/client.ts`; demo providers/fixtures `src/demo/`; screens `src/screens/`; shared activity UI `src/components/ExecutionTrace.tsx` and `LivingActivityCenter.tsx`. Demo mode uses local providers and must not issue `/api/` requests. Live mode uses same-origin API adapters with the saved tab-scoped bearer token when configured. The table below records current call paths and tests; mock tests are labelled separately from isolated local Python HTTP integration. The current isolated smoke uses temporary data and enforces authentication. It is not a production auth deployment test and does not exercise every route.

The complete 682-row route reference matrix below remains a static source-pattern inventory, not this wiring list. ## Direction A — frontend functions without complete backend support

All such UI remains in the approved React frontend. Each row gives design/demo/backend/wiring/test/proposed-work status. Missing backend functionality must be approved before implementation.

This current contract review contains **25 capability records** with mixed verified, partial, blocked and pending statuses. Persistent Live notification preferences are now wired to the existing project-scoped integration-preferences contract, and Agent start/history/cancel/retry is verified; the remaining trace-history limitation is recorded in its row. “Missing” means the current registered route contracts do not provide the operation as described, while a security-blocked row may have an existing route that is not safe to wire. This is not a proposal to build them.

| Workspace / UI function | Gap | Frontend source | Design | Demo | Route / handler | Backend + wiring | Permissions | Test evidence | Type | Proposed work | Priority | Blocker | Approval | Final status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Communications Inbox permanent delete in Live | Permanent delete is implemented in addition to reversible trash/restore. | `src/screens/AdvancedScreen.tsx` (`CommunicationsInbox`); `openagent/routes/communication_routes.py::handle_delete`; `openagent/communication.py::CommunicationHub.delete_message` | Implemented | Working locally | `DELETE /api/communication/messages/{message_id}?project=...`; route declaration in `openagent/routes/communication_routes.py::ROUTES` | Handler passes explicit project and authenticated owner to the service; the service removes only the matching row and records a `communication.message.deleted` audit event. | `communication:write`; explicit project and owner; missing/foreign record returns non-disclosing 404. ## Direction C — existing integrations requiring further verification

- Research and CodeQ declare SSE routes. Agents/Workflows lack a shared resumable event stream.
- CodeQ terminal output uses persisted line events and client polling while the child process runs. This is incremental polling, not SSE. `POST /api/terminal/stream` remains a synchronous compatibility operation.
- frontend-second commonly uses `project=default`; this is not a unified authorization boundary. CodeQ project IDs differ from enterprise workspace IDs.
- Communications inbox polling is separate from Calendar reminders. Calendar runtime starts a local worker that checks stored preferences and records due reminders; worker restart/dedup and external notification delivery remain unverified.
- Multi-user review: research records use project/session scope; conversation operations may resolve by opaque ID/project; agent approval should bind requester/project; verify share-expiry checks on every access path; define consistent project scope. Terminal owner/scope behavior has focused tests.
- Contract declaration does not prove handler behavior, response compatibility, auth, persistence or UI completion.

## Next work and approval boundary

1. Continue Milestone A desktop state implementation and parity work; current screenshot scores are below acceptance and the state matrix is not exhaustive.
2. After frontend acceptance, verify existing live functionality screen by screen with safe equivalent backend data, including errors, loading, permissions and persistence.
3. Review Direction A and B. Ask the user which missing backend additions or unexposed backend capabilities should be addressed; do not implement new backend capabilities before approval.
4. Keep `frontend/` and `frontend final.html` intact.

## Audit limitations

The source scan checks route-pattern text in production TS/JS and excludes tests and `src/demo`. Dynamic URL construction can be missed; found strings can be unused or belong to endpoint maps rather than an exercised control. Audience labels are triage aids, not authorization evidence. The screenshot metric is strict RGBA pixel equality and includes font rasterization differences. Selected backend tests used `unittest`; the complete backend suite and full live integration were not run.
